Two frames closing,\n *                  three opening, on the turn-away only.\n *   adaptation     the window is bright; turning toward it over-exposes for\n *                  a beat, then the eye settles.\n *   periphery      what is in the corner of the eye is soft. When turned, the\n *                  monitor side of the frame is defocused (R7-Q3: \"blurred to\n *                  the side when he looks in the corner\"). Never the page.\n *   vignette       always, faintly.\n *   drift          holds are stills; a head never is.\n *\n * Everything inside the camera wrapper - plate, screen, cards - moves together.\n */\n\nexport const HeadRoom: React.FC<{\n  pose: HeadPose;\n  frameW: number; frameH: number;\n  children: React.ReactNode;\n}> = ({ pose, frameW, frameH, children }) => {\n  const cam = poseCam(pose);\n  const k = turnK(pose);\n  const drift = driftAt(pose);\n  const src = staticFile(poseSrc(pose));\n\n  // Blink: frames 0..5 of the full turn-away. `brightness(${bright.toFixed(3)}) contrast(${contrast.toFixed(3)})` : undefined,\n          }}\n        >\n          <Img src={src} style={{ position: \"absolute\", inset: 0, width: \"100%\", height: \"100%\", objectFit: \"cover\" }} />\n          {periph > 0.01 && (\n            <Img\n              src={src}\n              style={{\n                position: \"absolute\", inset: 0, width: \"100%\", height: \"100%\", objectFit: \"cover\",\n                filter: `blur(${(6 * periph).toFixed(1)}px)`,\n                WebkitMaskImage: `linear-gradient(to right, rgba(0,0,0,${periph.toFixed(2)}) 0%, rgba(0,0,0,${(0.7 * periph).toFixed(2)}) 14%, rgba(0,0,0,0) 34%)`,\n                maskImage: `linear-gradient(to right, rgba(0,0,0,${periph.toFixed(2)}) 0%, rgba(0,0,0,${(0.7 * periph).toFixed(2)}) 14%, rgba(0,0,0,0) 34%)`,\n              }}\n            />\n          )}\n          {children}\n        </div>\n\n        {/* Vignette: the eye's own falloff. Faint, always. */}\n        <div\n          style={{\n            position: \"absolute\", inset: 0, pointerEvents: \"none\", zIndex: 20,\n            background: \"radial-gradient(ellipse at 50% 50%, rgba(0,0,0,0) 58%, rgba(0,0,0,0.16) 100%)\",\n          }}\n        />\n\n        {/* Blink: lids from above and below, soft edged. */}\n        {blink > 0.01 && (\n          <>\n            <div\n              style={{\n                position: \"absolute\", left: 0, right: 0, top: 0, height: `${(blink * 56).toFixed(1)}%`,\n                background: \"linear-gradient(to bottom, #050403 88%, rgba(5,4,3,0) 100%)\", zIndex: 30,\n              }}\n            />\n            <div\n              style={{\n                position: \"absolute\", left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0, height: `${(blink * 50).toFixed(1)}%`,\n                background: \"linear-gradient(to top, #050403 88%, rgba(5,4,3,0) 100%)\", zIndex: 30,\n              }}\n            />\n          </>\n        )}\n      </AbsoluteFill>\n    </CamCtx.Provider>\n  );\n};\n"}

Single source for every overlay's\n * place, so the layout can be reasoned about as a room, not as pixels.\n *\n * theta right (deg), phi down (deg), depth metres. theta 0 = monitor centre.\n * The monitor spans about -19..+15 deg horizontally and -13..+12 vertically.\n * Turned, the frame shows theta ~13..67: window and wall, monitor sliver at\n * the left edge. Column top-left. */\nexport const RECALL: Anchor = { theta: 48, phi: 3, depth: 1.55 };\nexport const RECALL_W = 1.0;\n\n/** Step label + cue while facing: a slim placard beside the monitor's right edge. */\nexport const PLACARD: Anchor = { theta: 21.5, phi: 4, depth: 0.98 };\nexport const PLACARD_W = 0.2;\n\n/** Intro cards (Q1): the head half-turns and the card stands right of the monitor. */\nexport const CARD: Anchor = { theta: 24, phi: -1.5, depth: 1.05 };\nexport const CARD_W = 0.4;\n"}

ROUND 7: seen through the eyes.\n *\n * The beat script still drives everything (picture and audio cannot drift).\n * What changed is WHERE the viewer is: a head that faces the page to read,\n * turns away to tell it back, and comes back to check. headState.ts turns the\n * beats into that head; HeadRoom shows the room from it; every overlay is an\n * object standing in that room (World.tsx), so nothing can sit on the page.\n *\n *   card     the head half-turns; the card stands beside the monitor.\n *   read     facing. Silent. The highlighter follows his real reading pace.\n *   tell     the look-away begins LEAD s before his first word.\n *   colour   still turned. \"Look Away\" hangs in the free space; what he says\n *            arrives as bubbles under it. The head returns for the next read.\n *   review   facing. The whole page swept once as a CHECK.\n *   press    facing. Space. The text goes.\n *   recall   turned for the whole recall; the argument rebuilds in the room.\n *   press2   back to face the page for the second press.\n */\nexport const POVInstructional: React.FC<{\n  slug: string; chunks: Chunk[]; script: Script; cues: Cue[]; recall: RecallSentence[];\n  audioSrc: string; where: string; qIndex: number; qTotal: number;\n}> = ({ chunks, script, cues, recall, audioSrc, where, qIndex, qTotal }) => {\n  const frame = useCurrentFrame();\n  const { fps, width, height, durationInFrames } = useVideoConfig();\n  const t = frame / fps;\n\n  const beat = script.beats.find((b) => t >= b.start && t < b.end) ?? script.beats[script.beats.length - 1];\n  const k = beat.end > beat.start ? (t - beat.start) / (beat.end - beat.start) : 1;\n\n  const events = React.useMemo(() => headEvents(script.beats), [script]);\n  const pose: HeadPose = poseAt(events, t);\n  const turned = turnK(pose);\n\n  const stepOf: Partial<Record<Beat[\"kind\"], StepKey>> = {\n    read: \"read\", tell: \"tell\", colour: \"colour\", review: \"review\",\n    press: \"recall\", recall: \"recall\", press2: \"recall\",\n  };\n  const step: StepKey | null = beat.kind === \"card\" ? beat.step! : (stepOf[beat.kind] ?? null);\n  const introduced = new Set(script.beats.filter((b) => b.kind === \"card\" && b.start < t).map((b) => b.step!));\n  const anyCards = script.beats.some((b) => b.kind === \"card\");\n  const labelOn = step !== null && beat.kind !== \"card\" && (step === \"review\" || !anyCards || introduced.has(step));\n  const beatFrames = Math.round((t - beat.start) * fps);\n\n  const recalling = beat.kind === \"recall\" || beat.kind === \"settle\" || beat.kind === \"press2\" ||\n    (beat.kind === \"press\" && k > 0.55);\n  const pressAnim = (kk: number) =>\n    interpolate(kk, [0.5, 0.58, 0.72, 0.8], [0, 1, 1, 0], { extrapolateLeft: \"clamp\", extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" });\n  const pressed = beat.kind === \"press\" || beat.kind === \"press2\" ? pressAnim(k) : 0;\n\n  // Captions, in ORIGINAL recording time (see Round 6 notes on `map`).\n  let srcT = -1;\n  let patchText: string | null = null;\n  if (beat.map) {\n    const into = t - beat.start;\n    for (const m of beat.map) {\n      if (into >= m.o && into < m.o + m.d) {\n        if (m.s === null) patchText = m.text ?? null; else srcT = m.s + (into - m.o);\n        break;\n      }\n    }\n  } else if (beat.srcStart !== undefined) {\n    srcT = beat.srcStart + (t - beat.start);\n  }\n  const cueIdx = srcT >= 0 ? cues.findIndex((c) => srcT >= c.start && srcT < c.end) : -1;\n  const cue = cueIdx >= 0 ? cues[cueIdx] : null;\n  const speaking = beat.kind === \"tell\" || beat.kind === \"colour\";\n  const bubbleText = patchText ?? (cue ? cue.text : null);\n  const bubbleFrom = patchText !== null\n    ? Math.round((t - beat.start) * fps)\n    : cue ? Math.round((srcT - cue.start) * fps) : 0;\n  // A bubble only exists in the space the head has turned toward.\n  const showBubble = speaking && !!bubbleText && turned > 0.6;\n\n  // Reading light: only while reading or checking; never while talking.\n  const activeNs = beat.chunkNs ?? [];\n  const light: ReadLight | null =\n    beat.kind === \"read\" ? { ns: activeNs, p: k, spread: 1.7 } :\n    beat.kind === \"review\" ? { ns: \"all\", p: Math.min(1, k * 1.06), spread: 3.4 } :\n    null;\n\n  // \"Look Away\": rises once the turn is well under way, holds while turned.\n  const awayEv = [...events].reverse().find((e) => e.move === \"away\" && e.t0 <= t);\n  const lookAwayFrames = awayEv ? Math.round((t - awayEv.t0 - 0.45) * fps) : -1;\n  const lookAwayOn = turned > 0.35 && pose.kind !== \"move\" || (pose.kind === \"move\" && pose.move === \"away\" && turned > 0.35);\n  const lookAwayEnter = lookAwayOn ? enterAt(lookAwayFrames, fps, 90) : 0;\n\n  // The Recall in the room.\n  const recallB = script.beats.find((b) => b.kind === \"recall\");\n  const inRecall = beat.kind === \"recall\" || beat.kind === \"press2\" || beat.kind === \"settle\";\n  const recallT = recallB ? t - recallB.start : -1;\n  const stackOn = inRecall && recallT >= 0 ? (beat.kind === \"settle\" ? stackLeave(k) : 1) : 0;\n\n  const facing = pose.kind === \"still\" && pose.name === \"facing\";\n  const through = Math.min(1, frame / durationInFrames);\n\n  return (\n    <AbsoluteFill>\n      <Audio src={staticFile(audioSrc)} />\n      <HeadRoom pose={pose} frameW={width} frameH={height}>\n        <MonitorPlane>\n          <Screen\n            through={through}\n            clock={mmss(t)}\n            where={`${where}  ·  ${qIndex} of ${qTotal}`}\n            instruction={recalling ? \"Say the whole argument back from memory.\" : \"Read it. Tell it back and colour it in as you go.\"}\n            keyDoes={recalling ? \"next stimulus\" : \"recall full stimulus\"}\n            pressed={pressed}\n          >\n            {recalling ? null : <Stimulus7 chunks={chunks} light={light} />}\n          </Screen>\n        </MonitorPlane>\n\n        {lookAwayEnter > 0.01 && <LookAway enter={lookAwayEnter} />}\n\n        {showBubble && bubbleText && (\n          <Bubble key={patchText !== null ? `patch-${beat.start}` : cueIdx} text={bubbleText} enter={enterAt(bubbleFrom, fps)} />\n        )}\n\n        {inRecall && recallT >= 0 && stackOn > 0 && turned > 0.5 && (\n          <RecallRoom sentences={recall} t={recallT} fps={fps} leave={stackOn} />\n        )}\n\n        {labelOn && step && facing && (\n          <Placard step={step} cue={!!beat.cue} enter={enterAt(beatFrames, fps)} />\n        )}\n\n        {beat.kind === \"card\" && beat.step && <IntroCard step={beat.step} k={k} />}\n      </HeadRoom>\n    </AbsoluteFill>\n  );\n};\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\LookAway.tsx", "content": "import React from \"react\";\nimport { interpolate, Easing } from \"remotion\";\nimport { WorldPlane } from \"./World\";\nimport { LOOK_AWAY, LOOK_AWAY_W } from \"./anchors\";\nimport { SEC } from \"../rrc/tokens\";\n\n/*\n * \"Look Away\". ROUND 7, his wording verbatim (R7-Q3).\n *\n * A sign hanging in the air in front of the curtain, where the head has just\n * turned to. It is in the ROOM (WorldPlane), so it arrives as the turn brings\n * it into view and slides out when the head returns; it never behaves like a\n * caption. Chrome-gradient serif italic with a stepped extrusion (layered\n * text-shadow, no filters) and a local bloom, the HyperFrames nameplate idea\n * applied to type. Words land one after the other.\n */\n\nconst EXTRUDE = 14;\nfunction extrusion(): string {\n  const s: string[] = [];\n  for (let i = 1; i <= EXTRUDE; i++) {\n    const a = 0.55 - (i / EXTRUDE) * 0.35;\n    s.push(`${i * 0.9}px ${i * 1.1}px 0 rgba(16,12,30,${a.toFixed(2)})`);\n  }\n  s.push(\"0 0 28px rgba(255,236,200,0.35)\", \"0 0 90px rgba(255,220,170,0.22)\");\n  return s.join(\", \");\n}\n\nconst Word: React.FC<{ text: string; enter: number; delay: number }> = ({ text, enter, delay }) => {\n  const e = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, (enter - delay) / (1 - delay)));\n  const y = interpolate(e, [0, 1], [70, 0], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });\n  const sc = interpolate(e, [0, 1], [0.86, 1], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });\n  return (\n    <span\n      style={{\n        display: \"inline-block\",\n        transform: `translateY(${y.toFixed(1)}px) scale(${sc.toFixed(3)})`,\n        opacity: interpolate(e, [0, 0.45], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" }),\n        backgroundImage: \"linear-gradient(178deg, #fffdf6 0%, #f4e9d2 42%, #b9a98a 70%, #f7efdf 100%)\",\n        WebkitBackgroundClip: \"text\", backgroundClip: \"text\",\n        color: \"transparent\",\n        textShadow: extrusion(),\n        marginRight: 34,\n      }}\n    >\n      {text}\n    </span>\n  );\n};\n\nexport const LookAway: React.FC<{ enter: number }> = ({ enter }) => (\n  <WorldPlane anchor={LOOK_AWAY} w={LOOK_AWAY_W} h={0.36} faceYaw={-9} faceTilt={3} zIndex={5}>\n    {/* local bloom, sits behind the letters */}\n    <div\n      style={{\n        position: \"absolute\", left: -80, top: -60, width: LOOK_AWAY_W * 1000 + 160, height: 480,\n        borderRadius: \"50%\",\n        background: \"radial-gradient(ellipse at 45% 55%, rgba(255,214,160,0.34) 0%, rgba(255,214,160,0.12) 45%, transparent 72%)\",\n        filter: \"blur(34px)\", opacity: Math.min(1, enter * 1.2),\n      }}\n    />\n    <div\n      style={{\n        position: \"absolute\", left: 0, top: 30, width: LOOK_AWAY_W * 1000,\n        fontFamily: SEC.serif, fontStyle: \"italic\", fontWeight: 500,\n        fontSize: 212, lineHeight: 1, letterSpacing: -4, whiteSpace: \"nowrap\",\n      }}\n    >\n      <Word text=\"Look\" enter={enter} delay={0} />\n      <Word text=\"Away\" enter={enter} delay={0.22} />\n    </div>\n  </WorldPlane>\n);\n"}

None of them is ever on the page,\n * because none of them is placed in frame pixels: each has a spot in the room\n * (anchors.ts) and the head decides where that lands.\n */\n\nconst GLASS_BG = \"linear-gradient(160deg, rgba(22,20,34,0.86) 0%, rgba(12,11,22,0.90) 100%)\";\nconst GLASS_EDGE = \"inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.28), inset 0 -1px 0 rgba(0,0,0,0.35), 0 30px 70px rgba(0,0,0,0.45)\";\n\nconst Glass: React.FC<{ radius?: number; pad?: string; children: React.ReactNode; style?: React.CSSProperties }> = ({\n  radius = 22, pad = \"22px 28px\", children, style,\n}) => (\n  <div\n    style={{\n      background: GLASS_BG, borderRadius: radius, padding: pad,\n      border: \"1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.16)\", boxShadow: GLASS_EDGE,\n      backdropFilter: \"blur(10px) saturate(1.1)\", WebkitBackdropFilter: \"blur(10px) saturate(1.1)\",\n      color: \"#fff\", fontFamily: SEC.sans, ...style,\n    }}\n  >\n    {children}\n  </div>\n);\n\nconst Bloom: React.FC<{ w: number; h: number; a?: number }> = ({ w, h, a = 0.28 }) => (\n  <div\n    style={{\n      position: \"absolute\", left: -w * 0.15, top: -h * 0.25, width: w * 1.3, height: h * 1.5, borderRadius: \"50%\",\n      background: `radial-gradient(ellipse at 40% 70%, rgba(150,170,255,${a}) 0%, rgba(150,170,255,${a * 0.3}) 48%, transparent 74%)`,\n      filter: \"blur(36px)\", pointerEvents: \"none\",\n    }}\n  />\n);\n\nfunction pop(enter: number) {\n  const lift = interpolate(enter, [0, 1], [40, 0], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });\n  const sc = interpolate(enter, [0, 1], [0.88, 1], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.back(1.4)) });\n  const op = interpolate(enter, [0, 0.35], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" });\n  return { transform: `translateY(${lift.toFixed(1)}px) scale(${sc.toFixed(3)})`, opacity: op, transformOrigin: \"20% 100%\" } as React.CSSProperties;\n}\n\n/** What he is saying. Bubbly: round, light, a tail back toward the speaker. */\nexport const Bubble: React.FC<{ text: string; enter: number }> = ({ text, enter }) => {\n  const W = BUBBLE_W * 1000;\n  return (\n    <WorldPlane anchor={BUBBLE} w={BUBBLE_W} h={0.34} faceYaw={-10} faceTilt={2} zIndex={6}>\n      <div style={{ position: \"absolute\", left: 0, top: 0, width: W, ...pop(enter) }}>\n        <Bloom w={W} h={220} />\n        <Glass radius={40} pad=\"26px 36px\" style={{ position: \"relative\", fontSize: 46, lineHeight: 1.3, letterSpacing: -0.3, fontWeight: 500 }}>\n          {text}\n          <div\n            style={{\n              position: \"absolute\", left: 54, bottom: -16, width: 34, height: 34, borderRadius: 6,\n              background: \"rgba(14,13,24,0.9)\", border: \"1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.14)\", borderTop: \"none\", borderLeft: \"none\",\n              transform: \"rotate(45deg)\",\n            }}\n          />\n        </Glass>\n      </div>\n    </WorldPlane>\n  );\n};\n\n/** The step, named while it happens, on a slim placard beside the monitor. */\nexport const Placard: React.FC<{ step: StepKey; cue: boolean; enter: number }> = ({ step, cue, enter }) => {\n  const s = STEPS[step];\n  const W = PLACARD_W * 1000;\n  return (\n    <WorldPlane anchor={PLACARD} w={PLACARD_W} h={0.34} faceYaw={-22} faceTilt={4} zIndex={5}>\n      <div style={{ position: \"absolute\", left: 0, top: 0, width: W, ...pop(enter) }}>\n        <Bloom w={W} h={200} a={0.22} />\n        <Glass radius={16} pad=\"16px 18px\" style={{ position: \"relative\" }}>\n          {s.n !== null && (\n            <div style={{ fontSize: 15, letterSpacing: 2.2, color: \"#a9bcff\", marginBottom: 6 }}>STEP {s.n}</div>\n          )}\n          <div style={{ fontFamily: SEC.serif, fontSize: 34, fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: -0.5, lineHeight: 1.05 }}>{s.name}</div>\n          {cue && (\n            <div style={{ marginTop: 10, fontSize: 18, lineHeight: 1.32, color: \"rgba(255,255,255,0.88)\" }}>{s.says}</div>\n          )}\n        </Glass>\n      </div>\n    </WorldPlane>\n  );\n};\n\n/** The film stopping to say what comes next. Stands beside the monitor. */\nexport const IntroCard: React.FC<{ step: StepKey; k: number }> = ({ step, k }) => {\n  const s = STEPS[step];\n  const W = CARD_W * 1000;\n  const enter = interpolate(k, [0.12, 0.3], [0, 1], { extrapolateLeft: \"clamp\", extrapolateRight: \"clamp\", easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });\n  const leave = interpolate(k, [0.8, 0.9], [1, 0], { extrapolateLeft: \"clamp\", extrapolateRight: \"clamp\", easing: Easing.in(Easing.cubic) });\n  const on = Math.min(enter, leave);\n  return (\n    <WorldPlane anchor={CARD} w={CARD_W} h={0.5} faceYaw={-20} faceTilt={3} zIndex={6}>\n      <div style={{ position: \"absolute\", left: 0, top: 0, width: W, ...pop(on) }}>\n        <Bloom w={W} h={330} a={0.32} />\n        <Glass radius={24} pad=\"26px 30px\" style={{ position: \"relative\" }}>\n          {s.n !== null && (\n            <div\n              style={{\n                display: \"inline-block\", fontSize: 15, letterSpacing: 2.4, color: \"#bcccff\",\n                padding: \"6px 12px\", borderRadius: 7, background: \"rgba(30,40,86,0.75)\", marginBottom: 14,\n              }}\n            >\n              STEP {s.n}\n            </div>\n          )}\n          <div style={{ fontFamily: SEC.serif, fontSize: 54, fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: -1.2, lineHeight: 1.02, marginBottom: 14 }}>\n            {s.name}\n          </div>\n          <div style={{ fontSize: 25, lineHeight: 1.4, color: \"rgba(255,255,255,0.92)\" }}>{s.says}</div>\n        </Glass>\n      </div>\n    </WorldPlane>\n  );\n};\n"}

ROUND 7.\n *\n * Same idea as Round 6's stack (the screen stays honestly blank; the argument\n * reassembles beside it, one sentence as he says it), but now it stands in the\n * free space the head has turned toward, under \"Look Away\", and it is a room\n * object, so the head's return carries it out of frame.\n */\n\nconst MIN_WORDS = 3;\n\nexport const RecallRoom: React.FC<{\n  sentences: RecallSentence[]; t: number; fps: number; leave: number;\n}> = ({ sentences, t, fps, leave }) => {\n  const lines = sentences.filter((s) => s.text.trim().split(/\\s+/).length >= MIN_WORDS);\n  const shown = lines.filter((s) => t >= s.start);\n  if (shown.length === 0) return null;\n  const W = RECALL_W * 1000;\n  // Size from the FULL line count so the column never shrinks under itself.\n  const AVAIL = 620;\n  let size = 40, gap = 12;\n  for (; size > 22; size--) {\n    const cpl = Math.max(20, Math.floor((W - 60) / (size * 0.5)));\n    const h = lines.reduce((a, s) => a + Math.ceil(s.text.length / cpl) * size * 1.3 + 26 + gap, 0);\n    if (h <= AVAIL) break;\n  }\n  return (\n    <WorldPlane anchor={RECALL} w={RECALL_W} h={0.75} faceYaw={-10} faceTilt={2} zIndex={6} opacity={leave}>\n      <div style={{ position: \"absolute\", left: 0, top: 0, width: W, display: \"flex\", flexDirection: \"column\", gap }}>\n        {shown.map((s, idx) => {\n          const e = enterAt(Math.round((t - s.start) * fps), fps, 96);\n          const age = shown.length - 1 - idx;\n          const dim = interpolate(Math.min(age, 5), [0, 5], [1, 0.78]);\n          return (\n            <div\n              key={s.index}\n              style={{\n                transform: `translateX(${interpolate(e, [0, 1], [40, 0]).toFixed(1)}px)`,\n                opacity: interpolate(e, [0, 0.4], [0, dim], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" }),\n              }}\n            >\n              <div\n                style={{\n                  display: \"inline-block\",\n                  background: \"linear-gradient(160deg, rgba(22,20,34,0.86), rgba(12,11,22,0.9))\",\n                  borderLeft: \"4px solid rgba(169,188,255,0.9)\",\n                  border: \"1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.14)\",\n                  borderLeftWidth: 4, borderLeftColor: \"rgba(169,188,255,0.9)\",\n                  borderRadius: \"6px 14px 14px 6px\",\n                  padding: `${Math.round(size * 0.35)}px ${Math.round(size * 0.6)}px`,\n                  fontFamily: SEC.sans, fontSize: size, lineHeight: 1.3, letterSpacing: -0.2, color: \"#fff\",\n                  boxShadow: \"inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.22), 0 22px 50px rgba(0,0,0,0.42)\",\n                }}\n              >\n                {s.text}\n              </div>\n            </div>\n          );\n        })}\n        {shown.length >= 2 && (\n          <div\n            style={{\n              alignSelf: \"flex-start\", marginTop: 6, fontFamily: SEC.sans, fontSize: 22, letterSpacing: 1.8, color: \"#a9bcff\",\n              background: \"rgba(9,11,26,0.72)\", borderRadius: 8, padding: \"8px 14px\",\n            }}\n          >\n            {shown.length} OF {lines.length} BACK\n          </div>\n        )}\n      </div>\n    </WorldPlane>\n  );\n};\n"}

ROUND 7.\n *\n * \"The highlight should be more in touch.\" Round 6's light was a loose glow\n * with a wide sigma; it hovered over the line. This is a marker stroke: it\n * sits ON the words, tight, warm, slightly uneven at its ends like a real pen,\n * and it advances word by word at his measured reading pace. Nothing is\n * dimmed or hidden; words behind the stroke stay fully legible (R6 rule).\n */\n\nexport const Stimulus7: React.FC<{ chunks: Chunk[]; light: ReadLight | null }> = ({ chunks, light }) => {\n  const litNs: number[] =\n    light === null ? [] : light.ns === \"all\" ? chunks.map((c) => c.n) : light.ns;\n  const runWords: { cn: number; wi: number }[] = [];\n  for (const c of chunks) {\n    if (!litNs.includes(c.n)) continue;\n    c.text.trim().split(/\\s+/).forEach((_, wi) => runWords.push({ cn: c.n, wi }));\n  }\n  const centre = light && runWords.length ? light.p * (runWords.length - 1) : -1;\n  // Tight: the stroke covers ~2 words either side of the eye, and everything\n  // already read keeps a faint trace so the path so far stays visible.\n  const sigma = Math.max(0.9, (light?.spread ?? 1.7) * 0.6);\n\n  const heatOf = (cn: number, wi: number): { h: number; trail: number } => {\n    if (centre < 0) return { h: 0, trail: 0 };\n    const idx = runWords.findIndex((r) => r.cn === cn && r.wi === wi);\n    if (idx < 0) return { h: 0, trail: 0 };\n    const d = (idx - centre) / sigma;\n    const h = Math.exp(-d * d);\n    const trail = idx < centre ? 1 : 0;\n    return { h, trail };\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <div\n      style={{\n        maxWidth: \"52ch\", color: SEC.ink, fontFamily: SEC.serif,\n        fontSize: SEC.stimulusPx, fontWeight: 400, lineHeight: SEC.stimulusLh,\n        fontOpticalSizing: \"none\", fontVariationSettings: '\"opsz\" 14',\n      }}\n    >\n      {chunks.map((c) => (\n        <span key={c.n}>\n          {c.text.trim().split(/\\s+/).map((w, wi) => {\n            const { h, trail } = heatOf(c.n, wi);\n            const a = Math.max(0.5 * h, 0.16 * trail);\n            const on = a > 0.03;\n            return (\n              <span\n                key={wi}\n                style={on ? {\n                  backgroundImage: `linear-gradient(to bottom, transparent 8%, rgba(255,214,64,${a.toFixed(3)}) 8%, rgba(255,214,64,${a.toFixed(3)}) 92%, transparent 92%)`,\n                  boxShadow: h > 0.3 ? `0 0 ${(6 * h).toFixed(1)}px ${(2 * h).toFixed(1)}px rgba(255,204,60,${(0.28 * h).toFixed(3)})` : undefined,\n                  borderRadius: \"3px 6px 4px 5px\",\n                  margin: \"0 -1px\", padding: \"0 1px\",\n                } : undefined}\n              >\n                {w}{\" \"}\n              </span>\n            );\n          })}\n        </span>\n      ))}\n    </div>\n  );\n};\n"}

Both 21:9 outpaints were unusable as glue (their left\n  bookshelf strip was salvaged as a peripheral wing). 35 credits per 4k image.\n- SIFT gives ~400 keypoints on a smooth generated interior; the pair could not\n  be matched automatically. The chrome\n  face and the extrusion must be two stacked copies of the text.\n- The head must not snap between poses: `headState.ts` treats tell/colour/recall\n  as one turned RUN and only turns at its edges. Q1's colour card sits inside\n  that run, so the head returns for the card and turns away again after it.\n- `distanceTransformWithLabels` is the right fill for uncovered panorama\n  corners; a row-wise extension leaves black bands where no valid pixel exists\n  above.\nEOF\necho ok", "description": "Document Round 7 in the README"}

## assistant

Only the 4K renders remain, and they're running detached with a waiter armed. Nothing else is independent of them, so I'll report when they land.

**Where the video is:** the Round 7 Q1 cut is delivered above as a 720p file (the 1080p at 94 MB was too large for the phone upload, so it lives at `projects/video/out/r7/instr-q1-1080.mp4` on the desktop). 4K versions of all three cuts are rendering now.

**What changed since Round 6, all from your grill answers:**
- **Real head turn.** The room is a cylindrical panorama of the same plate plus one generated right-hand view, stitched on the door's white mullion. The head faces the centred screen to read, turns 40° right to tell it back, and comes back. Neck parallax, shutter blur, a gaze-shift blink, and exposure adaptation toward the window are all in.
- **Screen centred.** Facing view has the monitor dead centre with bookshelf and curtain either side.
- **Nothing on the page.** Every card, bubble, and the Recall are objects in the room, projected through the same camera as the plate. Step cards stand beside the monitor during a half turn.
- **"Look Away"** is a chrome serif sign hanging in front of the curtain, your wording.
- **Tighter highlight.** A marker stroke on the words, not a glow over the line.

**One thing to flag:** Kling charged 35 credits per 4K image, so the five reference generations cost 175 credits, over the 150 cap I set myself. 466 remain. Two of the five were unusable as intended (the outpaints re-render the room), though one gave the bookshelf strip on the left.

**Not verified by anyone's eye yet:** whether 40° reads as "not too dramatic", whether the bezel sliver at the frame edge should stay or go, and whether the blink and exposure lift feel natural rather than like effects. Those are your calls once you've watched it.

import type React from "react";
import { ArrowRight } from "lucide-react";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import { renderInlineMarks } from "../prompt-format";
import "./section-launch.css";

export interface SectionBriefingPageProps {
  title: string;
  subtitle: string | null;
  instructions: ActivityInstructions;
  onStart: () => void;
  onExit?: () => void;
}

/**
 * The instructions, on their own page, before anything records.
 *
 * Pressing Start on a section used to open the microphone straight away, and
 * the method was folded into tabs on a page she had already scrolled past. This
 * page is the whole briefing laid out in order, every step numbered and open,
 * with one Start button at the top and the same one at the foot. The written
 * instructions sit under it, closed. Nothing here begins the run.
 */
export function SectionBriefingPage({
  title,
  subtitle,
  instructions,
  onStart,
  onExit,
}: SectionBriefingPageProps) {
  const briefing = instructions.briefing;
  const steps = briefing?.sections.find((s) => (s.steps?.length ?? 0) > 0)?.steps ?? [];

  const startButton = (label: string) => (
    <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
      {label}
      <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
    </button>
  );

  return (
    <div className="sl sl-briefing-page">
      <header className="sl-bp-head">
        <div>
          {/* No chip. The title already names the drill, and on the door the
              chip earns its place because the H1 there is the bare name. */}
          <h1 className="sl-title">{title}</h1>
          {(instructions.tagline || subtitle) && (
            <p className="sl-tagline">
              {instructions.tagline ? renderInlineMarks(instructions.tagline, "sl-bp-tag") : subtitle}
            </p>
          )}
        </div>
        <div className="sl-cta">
          {startButton("Start the section")}
          {onExit && (
            <button type="button" className="sl-brief" onClick={onExit}>
              Back
            </button>
          )}
        </div>
      </header>

      {/* One fact, the one that lowers her shoulders before she reads. That the
          section records in one take, and what pausing does and does not buy
          her, is said at the foot, beside
          the Start she presses after reading; saying it here as well made the
          notice a paragraph she skipped. (An earlier version of this comment
          claimed Step 4 carried it. It did not: the sentence lived only in
          `instructions.rules`, which this page never renders, so for a day the
          page did not say it anywhere.) */}
      {/* No role="status". A live region is for state that changes without a page
          load; this sentence is the same every time, and announcing it as an
          update is noise. */}
      <p className="sl-notice">
        Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start.
      </p>

      {steps.length > 0 && (
        <section className="sl-bp-steps" aria-label="What you do, step by step">
          <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">What you do, step by step</h2>
          <ol className="sl-bp-list">
            {steps.map((step, i) => (
              <li key={i} style={{ "--i": i } as React.CSSProperties}>
                <span className="sl-bp-n">Step {i + 1}</span>
                <strong>{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-bps-l-${i}`)}</strong>
                <p>{renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-bps-b-${i}`)}</p>
              </li>
            ))}
          </ol>
        </section>
      )}

      {/* The four steps above ARE the instruction. This is the same method told
          again at length, and rendering both open left her reading the method
          twice with no way to tell which one she was meant to follow. Closed by
          default, one click from anyone who wants the long version. */}
      {briefing && (
        <details className="sl-bp-more">
          {/* The summary carries a real heading rather than only looking like one.
              It read as a section title and was absent from the heading outline,
              which is what left the page jumping H2 to H4. */}
          <summary>
            <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">Read the long version</h2>
          </summary>
          <section className="sl-essay sl-bp-essay" aria-label="Full instructions">
          {briefing.lede && <p className="rd-lede">{renderInlineMarks(briefing.lede, "sl-bp-lede")}</p>}
          {briefing.sections.map((section, i) => (
            <section key={i}>
              <h3>{renderInlineMarks(section.heading, `sl-bp-h-${i}`)}</h3>
              {section.body.map((para, j) => (
                <p key={j}>{renderInlineMarks(para, `sl-bp-p-${i}-${j}`)}</p>
              ))}
              {section.steps && (
                <ol>
                  {section.steps.map((step, j) => (
                    <li key={j}>
                      <strong>{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-bp-sl-${i}-${j}`)}</strong>{" "}
                      {renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-bp-sb-${i}-${j}`)}
                    </li>
                  ))}
                </ol>
              )}
              {section.demo && (
                <ol>
                  {section.demo.beats.map((b, j) => (
                    <li key={j}>
                      <strong>{b.kind}</strong> {renderInlineMarks(b.text, `sl-bp-d-${i}-${j}`)}
                    </li>
                  ))}
                </ol>
              )}
            </section>
          ))}
          </section>
        </details>
      )}

      {/* The two facts she needs at the moment she presses Start, said where
          she presses it: what a pause costs her, and what the time is for. The
          closer is the authored last beat before Start and belongs here too. */}
      <footer className="sl-bp-foot">
        <p className="sl-bp-fact">
          It runs in one take. You can pause: the text hides and the clock stops
          while you are away, and you cannot go back to a stimulus. Twenty-five
          minutes for the whole section is the goal you are working toward, not a
          limit.
        </p>
        {briefing?.closer && (
          <p className="sl-bp-closer">{renderInlineMarks(briefing.closer, "sl-bp-closer")}</p>
        )}
        {/* Says what happens to her, not how the clock works. "Counting up"
            described the mechanism and left her wondering what it counted
            toward. */}
        <p>
          When you are ready, press Start. Recording begins the moment you do, and the timer
          starts with it. The first time, your browser will ask to use your microphone. Allow it.
        </p>
        <div className="sl-cta">{startButton("Start the section")}</div>
      </footer>
    </div>
  );
}

Tool result:
# Copy corrections

Every student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before
writing or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.

Companion to [[teaching-voice]], which says how the writing should sound. This
says what has actually gone wrong and why.

Started 2026-09-07 from a Read, Recall, Colour pass. Add to it every time a
correction is made.

## The rules these corrections came from

Three rules already existed and all three leaked anyway, because each was
enforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface
added later starts uncovered.

- **No em dashes, ever.** [[teaching-voice]], Core voice.
- **No internal vocabulary in student copy.** `projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`
  carries the glossary and marks what to avoid.
- **Every CSS token a stylesheet uses must be defined in it.** `projects/lsatu/CLAUDE.md`.

Guards now live with the content rather than with each component:
`content/instructions-voice.test.ts` checks the authored instructions, so every
surface that renders them is covered, including surfaces not written yet.

## Corrections

### "Recall Rephrase Colour" → "Read, Recall, Colour"
The door's heading was hardcoded to a name the product does not have, one line
under a chip reading "Read, Recall, Colour". "Rephrase" belongs to a different
Activity, Conditional Rephrase. A test asserted the wrong name, which is why
2,600 passing tests never caught it.
**Lesson:** when a test pins a string, check the string is right, not just that
it matches.

### "Intake only. Nothing comes back out yet…" → plain instruction
Clinical and abstract, and the sentence then told her to read "with full
intonation" while step one is silent reading. The clause that made intonation
internal, "hear it naturally in your head", had been dropped in an earlier edit.
**Lesson:** a word like "intake" reads as jargon. And when copy is trimmed,
check what the trimmed clause was load-bearing for.

### "Three beats through the stimulus" → "Three steps"
"Beat" is the internal code name for one of the four method steps. The glossary
says students see **step**. The interface ten pixels below labelled the same four
things Step 1 to Step 4, so the page used two names for one thing.
**Lesson:** never let an implementation name reach the page.

### "22 pieces to read" → line removed
A **piece** is one chunk, roughly a sentence, and the instructions on that same
page use the word that way. The count was of stimuli, so "22 pieces" read as 22
sentences.
**Lesson:** a unit word must mean one thing per surface. Check what a word
already means on the page before reusing it.

### "say it back" → "tell it back", "The Read" → "The Recall"
The library door still carried both retired names. "The Read" was step four's
former name and collided with step one, which is why it was renamed.
**Lesson:** a rename is not finished until every row carries it. The door was
----
---
title: Teaching Voice
type: concept
tags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, content]
created: 2026-06-10
updated: 2026-07-18
status: active
subject: Teaching Voice
aliases: []
---

# Teaching Voice

How LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.

It replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools "preserve verbatim" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.

## Core voice (every register)

The constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.

- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.
- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student's own history. Praise is a specific observation, not "great job."
- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard ("expect this to be exhausting and very challenging"). Never be blunt about a student's shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.
- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: "partner with you on your LSAT journey," "getting set up," "foundational." The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.
- **Balanced rhythm.** Short declaratives plus one slightly longer warm beat. Air between ideas. Each idea gets its own line. Real messages are short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters).
- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype ("unlock," "crush," "level up," "game-changer" — "crush" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers ("Sorry for such a long delay!"). He is not exclamation-averse.
- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what's ahead.
- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.

## Openings, closings, and signature phrases

The mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.

- **Greeting.** "Hi [Name]," is the default (used ~519x). "Hey [Name]," for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). "Hello [Name]," (~105x) and "Dear [Name]," (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.
- **Sign-off.** "Best," and "Best regards," are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). "Thanks," when it's a favor or logistics (~146x). "Warmly," / "Warm regards," (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. "Talk soon," (~9x) for someone he'll see again soon.
- **Signature.** Almost always signed **"Joshua"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.
- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don't overuse):** "let me know" (by far the most common, ~390x), "looking forward," "I hope" / "hope all is well" / "hope you," "no worries at all," "happy to" / "more than happy to," "just wanted to make sure," "make sure," "no rush," "of course."
- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs — never.)

import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { SectionBriefingPage } from "./SectionBriefingPage";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import sectionInstructions from "../content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json";

/**
 * The instructions page, and the three ways it went wrong.
 *
 * It arrived without any of the guards the four older surfaces carry, which is
 * exactly how an em dash and a miscounted unit reached a student.
 */
function renderPage(props?: Partial<Parameters<typeof SectionBriefingPage>[0]>) {
  return render(
    <SectionBriefingPage
      title="Read, Recall, Colour · PT1 Section 3"
      subtitle="A whole section, out loud, against the clock"
      instructions={sectionInstructions as ActivityInstructions}
      onStart={vi.fn()}
      {...props}
    />,
  );
}

describe("the section briefing page", () => {
  it("says at the foot, beside Start, what one take and a pause each cost her, and what the time is for", () => {
    renderPage();
    const foot = document.querySelector(".sl-bp-foot")?.textContent ?? "";
    expect(foot).toMatch(/one take/i);
    // The runner ships a Pause button, so the foot says what pausing does
    // rather than denying it. Joshua's call, 2026-09-09.
    expect(foot).toMatch(/you can pause/i);
    expect(foot).toMatch(/the clock stops/i);
    expect(foot).toMatch(/cannot go back/i);
    expect(foot).toMatch(/goal/i);
    // The authored closer is the last beat before Start, and it renders here.
    expect(foot).toContain("One a day holds you steady.");
  });

  it("carries no em dash, which Joshua never types", () => {
    renderPage();
    expect(document.body.textContent ?? "").not.toMatch(/[—–]/);
  });

  it("carries no meta line: the title names the section and nothing else belongs there", () => {
    renderPage();
    expect(document.querySelector(".sl-bp-meta")).toBeNull();
    const text = document.body.textContent ?? "";
    expect(text).not.toMatch(/\d+ stimuli/);
    expect(text).not.toMatch(/about \d+ min/);
    // The count and the clock were the whole line, and both were noise here.
    // The clock is stated where it matters, at the foot and in the long version.
    expect(text).not.toContain("pieces to read");
  });

  it("keeps the internal writing vocabulary off the page", () => {
    renderPage();
    // CONTEXT.md: "Beat" is the internal code term for one of the four method
    // steps. Students see "step". The door already forbids these words, but the
    // door never renders the written instructions and this page does, so
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import instructions from "./read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json";

/**
 * The house rules for student-facing copy, checked against the content itself.
 *
 * These rules already existed. They were enforced per component, so each new
 * surface started uncovered, and in one night three separate rules leaked
 * through the same new page: an em dash, "Three beats through the stimulus",
 * and a title the product does not have. The door's own guard could not have
 * caught the second one even in principle, because the door never renders the
 * written instructions.
 *
 * So the guard belongs to the words, not to whichever component draws them.
 * Every surface that renders this file is covered by this test, including the
 * ones not written yet.
 *
 * `coach` is deliberately exempt: it is the brief handed to the grader, not
 * copy a student reads, and it needs the precise internal vocabulary.
 */

/** Internal names, from the glossary in `../CONTEXT.md`. */
const INTERNAL =
  /\b(beat|beats|briefing|intake|phase|tab|tabs|card|cards|chunk|chunks)\b/i;

/** Retired names the product has moved off. */
const RETIRED = /\b(say it back|the read\b|reading recall|CLIR|quip)\b/i;

function walk(node: unknown, path = ""): [string, string][] {
  if (typeof node === "string") return [[path, node]];
  if (Array.isArray(node)) return node.flatMap((v, i) => walk(v, `${path}/${i}`));
  if (node && typeof node === "object") {
    return Object.entries(node).flatMap(([k, v]) => walk(v, `${path}/${k}`));
  }
  return [];
}

const studentFacing = walk(instructions).filter(([p]) => !p.startsWith("/coach"));

describe("the Read, Recall, Colour instructions, as a student reads them", () => {

.sl .sl-start:focus-visible,
.sl .sl-brief:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rd-ink);
  outline-offset: 3px;
}
.rd-hero .sl-start:focus-visible,
.rd-hero .sl-brief:focus-visible {
  outline-color: var(--rd-cream);
}
/* 43x44 was under the 44x44 floor, and this is the exit control. */
.sl-brief {
  min-width: 44px;
}

/* Two more hero classes drawn in white for the door's dark panel. On paper they
   were invisible: the drill's own name, and a Back button nobody could see. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-chip {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  background: var(--rd-paper);
  border-color: var(--rd-line);
  backdrop-filter: none;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-brief {
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
  text-decoration-color: var(--rd-line);
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-brief:hover {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  text-decoration-color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
/* A heading inside the long version was set larger than the section heading
   above it, so the page read as if the essay outranked its own label. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-essay h3 {
  font-size: 20px;
  margin-top: 28px;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-title {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  font-size: clamp(30px, 3.4vw, 46px);
  line-height: 1.06;
  letter-spacing: -0.02em;
  margin: 14px 0 0;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-title em {
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
/* .sl-tagline is white with a drop shadow, which is right on the door's dark
   hero and unreadable here: this page sits on paper. The briefing page reuses
   the class for the same line of copy, so it restates the colour rather than
   forking the class. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-tagline {
  margin: 12px 0 0;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
  text-shadow: none;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-tagline strong {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
/* The long version, closed by default. */
.sl-bp-more > summary {
  cursor: pointer;
  list-style: none;
.sl-bp-h2 {
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 12px;
  font-weight: 500;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
  margin-bottom: 16px;
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
.sl-bp-list {
  display: grid;
  gap: 14px;
  list-style: none;
  padding: 0;
  margin: 0;
}
.sl-bp-list li {
  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line, hsl(var(--border)));
  border-radius: 16px;
  padding: 18px 20px;
}
.sl-bp-n {
  display: block;
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 11px;
  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
  margin-bottom: 6px;
}
.sl-bp-list li strong {
  display: block;
  font-size: 18px;
  margin-bottom: 6px;
}
/* The sentences she has to follow ran past 100 characters a line. The essay
   already holds 68ch; the steps get a measure too. */
.sl-bp-list li p {
  margin: 0;
  line-height: 1.6;
  max-width: 62ch;
}
.sl-bp-foot p {
  margin: 0;
  max-width: 62ch;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
.sl-bp-fact {
  font-size: 17px;
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
.sl-bp-foot .sl-bp-fact {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
.sl-bp-closer {
   where the eye can tell apart three. One scale, in rem so it follows her
   browser setting, ratio about 1.2 between neighbours:
     label 0.6875  body 1  lead 1.1875  step 1.375  title clamp(2, 3.4vw, 3)
   Roles: mono for labels, Inter for anything she reads at length, Fraunces
   for the lines that are spoken to her (title, step names, lede, closer). */
.sl-briefing-page {
  --bp-label: 0.6875rem;
  --bp-body: 1rem;
  --bp-lead: 1.1875rem;
  --bp-step: 1.375rem;
  font-size: var(--bp-body);
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-title {
  font-size: clamp(2rem, 3.4vw, 3rem);
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-tagline {
  font-size: var(--bp-lead);
  line-height: 1.5;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-notice,
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-fact {
  font-size: var(--bp-lead);
  line-height: 1.5;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-h2,
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-n {
  font-size: var(--bp-label);
}
/* Step names in the display face: they are the four things said to her,
   and the sans bold they were in made them read as form labels. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-list li strong {
  font-family: var(--rd-display);
  font-weight: 500;
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 24, "SOFT" 30, "WONK" 0;
  font-size: var(--bp-step);
  line-height: 1.2;
  letter-spacing: -0.01em;
  margin-bottom: 8px;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-list li p {
  font-size: var(--bp-body);
  line-height: 1.65;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-foot p {
  font-size: var(--bp-body);
  line-height: 1.6;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-closer {
  font-size: var(--bp-step);
  line-height: 1.3;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-essay p {
  font-size: var(--bp-body);
  line-height: 1.7;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-essay p.rd-lede {
  font-size: var(--bp-step);
  line-height: 1.4;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-essay h3 {
  font-size: var(--bp-step);
  margin: 32px 0 10px;
}
@media (max-width: 640px) {
  .sl-bp-head {
    flex-direction: column;
  }
}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu-wt-rrc-demos\\src\\features\\activity-centre\\content\\read-recall-colour-section.demos.ts", "content": "/**\n * The worked demonstrations on the Read, Recall, Colour briefing page.\n *\n * Three stimuli from one real section, each run the way the four steps\n * describe, filmed from the student's own eyes: face the page to read, turn\n * away to tell it back, come back to check, then the whole argument from\n * memory. Built in `projects/video` (Round 7, 2026-09-10); the files live in\n * `public/rrc-demos/` like the other bootcamp videos.\n *\n * The label is assembled from the numbers, never typed, so a new demo cannot\n * arrive with a mismatched caption.\n */\nexport interface RrcDemo {\n  /** Stable id, also the file stem. */\n  id: string;\n  prepTest: number;\n  section: number;\n  question: number;\n  src: string;\n  poster: string;\n  /** Seconds, for the little duration mark. */\n  seconds: number;\n}\n\nexport const RRC_DEMOS: RrcDemo[] = [\n  { id: \"pt105-s2-q1\", prepTest: 105, section: 2, question: 1, src: \"/rrc-demos/rrc-demo-pt105-s2-q1.mp4\", poster: \"/rrc-demos/rrc-demo-pt105-s2-q1.jpg\", seconds: 140 },\n  { id: \"pt105-s2-q2\", prepTest: 105, section: 2, question: 2, src: \"/rrc-demos/rrc-demo-pt105-s2-q2.mp4\", poster: \"/rrc-demos/rrc-demo-pt105-s2-q2.jpg\", seconds: 142 },\n  { id: \"pt105-s2-q3\", prepTest: 105, section: 2, question: 3, src: \"/rrc-demos/rrc-demo-pt105-s2-q3.mp4\", poster: \"/rrc-demos/rrc-demo-pt105-s2-q3.jpg\", seconds: 110 },\n];\n\nexport function demoLabel(d: RrcDemo): string {\n  return `Demo · PrepTest ${d.prepTest} · Section ${d.section} · Question ${d.question}`;\n}\n\nexport function demoDuration(d: RrcDemo): string {\n  const m = Math.floor(d.seconds / 60);\n  const s = d.seconds % 60;\n  return `${m}:${String(s).padStart(2, \"0\")}`;\n}\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu-wt-rrc-demos\\src\\features\\activity-centre\\components\\SectionDemos.tsx", "content": "import { RRC_DEMOS, demoDuration, demoLabel, type RrcDemo } from \"../content/read-recall-colour-section.demos\";\n\n/**\n * The four steps, watched being done.\n *\n * Three stimuli from a real section, each run the way the steps above say,\n * filmed from the student's own eyes: face the page to read, turn away to\n * tell it back and react, come back to check, then the whole argument from\n * memory with the text gone. The caption on each is its practice test,\n * section and question, assembled from the numbers so it cannot drift.\n *\n * Nothing autoplays. The page is quiet until she presses play, the same rule\n * as the microphone: nothing begins without her.\n */\nexport function SectionDemos({ demos = RRC_DEMOS }: { demos?: RrcDemo[] }) {\n  if (demos.length === 0) return null;\n  return (\n    <section className=\"sl-bp-demos\" aria-labelledby=\"sl-bp-demos-h\">\n      <h2 className=\"sl-bp-h2\" id=\"sl-bp-demos-h\">\n        Watch it done\n      </h2>\n      <p className=\"sl-bp-demos-lede\">\n        Three stimuli from one real section, each worked exactly as the steps above describe. Listen\n        for the look away, the retell in plain words, and the whole argument coming back with the text\n        gone.\n      </p>\n      <ul className=\"sl-bp-demo-list\">\n        {demos.map((d) => (\n          <li key={d.id} className=\"sl-bp-demo\">\n            <div className=\"sl-bp-demo-frame\">\n              <video\n                controls\n                preload=\"metadata\"\n                playsInline\n                poster={d.poster}\n                aria-label={demoLabel(d)}\n              >\n                <source src={d.src} type=\"video/mp4\" />\n              </video>\n            </div>\n            <div className=\"sl-bp-demo-cap\">\n              <span className=\"sl-bp-demo-label\">{demoLabel(d)}</span>\n              <span className=\"sl-bp-demo-time\">{demoDuration(d)}</span>\n            </div>\n          </li>\n        ))}\n      </ul>\n    </section>\n  );\n}\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu-wt-rrc-demos\\src\\features\\activity-centre\\components\\SectionBriefingPage.demos.test.tsx", "content": "import { render } from \"@testing-library/react\";\nimport { describe, expect, it, vi } from \"vitest\";\nimport { SectionBriefingPage } from \"./SectionBriefingPage\";\nimport type { ActivityInstructions } from \"../api/types\";\nimport sectionInstructions from \"../content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json\";\nimport { RRC_DEMOS, demoLabel } from \"../content/read-recall-colour-section.demos\";\n\n/**\n * The demonstrations on the briefing page (2026-09-10).\n *\n * Three real stimuli, each captioned with its practice test, section and\n * question. The caption is assembled from numbers, so the test checks the\n * assembly, and the house rules that leaked through this page once before.\n */\nfunction renderPage() {\n  return render(\n    <SectionBriefingPage\n      title=\"Read, Recall, Colour · PT1 Section 3\"\n      subtitle={null}\n      instructions={sectionInstructions as ActivityInstructions}\n      onStart={vi.fn()}\n    />,\n  );\n}\n\ndescribe(\"the demonstrations on the briefing page\", () => {\n  it(\"shows one player per demo, captioned with its practice test, section and question\", () => {\n    renderPage();\n    const videos = document.querySelectorAll(\".sl-bp-demo video\");\n    expect(videos.length).toBe(RRC_DEMOS.length);\n    const text = document.querySelector(\".sl-bp-demos\")?.textContent ?? \"\";\n    for (const d of RRC_DEMOS) {\n      expect(text).toContain(`PrepTest ${d.prepTest} · Section ${d.section} · Question ${d.question}`);\n      expect(demoLabel(d)).toMatch(/^Demo · PrepTest \\d+ · Section \\d+ · Question \\d+$/);\n    }\n  });\n\n  it(\"never autoplays and never loops: nothing begins without her\", () => {\n    renderPage();\n    for (const v of Array.from(document.querySelectorAll<HTMLVideoElement>(\".sl-bp-demo video\"))) {\n      expect(v.hasAttribute(\"autoplay\")).toBe(false);\n      expect(v.hasAttribute(\"loop\")).toBe(false);\n      expect(v.hasAttribute(\"controls\")).toBe(true);\n      expect(v.getAttribute(\"preload\")).toBe(\"metadata\");\n    }\n  });\n\n  it(\"sits between the steps and the long version\", () => {\n    renderPage();\n    const steps = document.querySelector(\".sl-bp-steps\");\n    const demos = document.querySelector(\".sl-bp-demos\");\n    const more = document.querySelector(\".sl-bp-more\");\n    expect(steps && demos && more).toBeTruthy();\n    expect(steps!.compareDocumentPosition(demos!) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING).toBeTruthy();\n    expect(demos!.compareDocumentPosition(more!) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING).toBeTruthy();\n  });\n\n  it(\"keeps the house rules: no em dash, no internal vocabulary\", () => {\n    renderPage();\n    const text = document.querySelector(\".sl-bp-demos\")?.textContent ?? \"\";\n    expect(text).not.toMatch(/[—–]/);\n    expect(text).not.toMatch(/\\b(beat|beats|card|cards|chunk|chunks|intake|phase)\\b/i);\n  });\n});\n"}

Nothing autoplays: the page is quiet until she\npresses play, the same rule as the microphone. Three across on desktop, two\nat 900px, one on a phone; every token from section-launch.css.\n\nGuarded like the rest of the page: the demos test checks the caption\nassembly, the no-autoplay rule, the placement, and the house rules (no em\ndash, no internal vocabulary).\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" && git push -q -u origin feat/rrc-demo-videos 2>&1 | tail -2 && gh pr create --title \"RRC briefing: three worked demonstrations, captioned by test, section and question\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## What\nAdds a **Watch it done** block to the Read, Recall, Colour briefing page: three video demonstrations (PrepTest 105, Section 2, Questions 1 to 3), each captioned `Demo · PrepTest 105 · Section 2 · Question N`, sitting between the four steps and the long version.\n\nThe films are the Round 7 first-person cuts from `projects/video`: face the page to read, turn away to tell it back and react, come back to check, then the whole argument from memory with the text gone.\n\n## How\n- `content/read-recall-colour-section.demos.ts`: the list, with the caption assembled from numbers so it cannot drift.\n- `components/SectionDemos.tsx`: the block. `controls`, `preload=\"metadata\"`, poster, no autoplay, no loop.\n- `section-launch.css`: three across, two at 900px, one at 640px; ## user

Tool result:
---
name: lovable-frontend-publish-is-separate
description: A push builds a Lovable preview but never reaches lsatprep.study; publish with deploy_project, wait for the sync, and prove it from the bundle.
metadata:
  type: project
---

Pushing to `main` makes Lovable build the commit — its project record shows a
preview screenshot tagged with the commit sha within minutes — but the published
site at lsatprep.study keeps serving the previous bundle until something calls
`deploy_project` (Lovable MCP, `project_id 53b9a6f2-aba7-4ae4-8dd1-fa5b8aab4d76`,
name `lsatu`). Confirmed 2026-09-08: a fix sat built-but-unpublished for 20
minutes with no error anywhere.

**The sync lags the push.** `deploy_project` publishes whatever Lovable has
ingested, not what is on origin. On 2026-09-09 a deploy fired seconds after a
successful push and shipped the *previous* commit; `get_project` showed
`latest_commit_sha` catching up about three minutes later, and a second deploy
was needed. There is no error and no warning — the deploy reports `pending` and
succeeds, just on the wrong commit.

**Why:** build, sync and publish are three separate steps, and none of them
reports that the others have not happened. A green push reads as shipped.

**How to apply:** before calling `deploy_project`, call `get_project` and check
`latest_commit_sha` equals your HEAD; if it does not, wait and re-check. After
deploying, prove it from the bundle: pick a string **the commit alone**
introduces — not a class name an earlier deploy already shipped, which is how a
stale bundle passed a check on 2026-09-09 — then fetch `https://lsatprep.study/`,
read the `index-*` asset hash out of it, and grep the asset for the marker.
Allow several minutes; the hash changing is the signal a publish landed at all.
Note `deploy_project` ships every unpublished commit on main, not just yours.

Same trap, different half: [[lovable-edge-functions-do-not-deploy-from-push]].
Related: [[push-only-your-own-commits]].

Three across on desktop, one column on a phone.

- Guards: a new test for the captions, no-autoplay rule, placement, and house copy rules. 20 tests pass, type-check clean.

**Two things to know**
- A Lovable plugin rewrote `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts` during install in the worktree and it slipped into my first commit. You declined the force push, so a second commit restored the file before the merge. Nothing of that file changed on main.
- The heading isn't in the main JS bundle because that page is code-split, so the proof of the publish is the demo file returning 200 and the page itself rendering in your browser.

The Chrome tab is left open on the page for you. The worktree at `projects/lsatu-wt-rrc-demos` is still there if you want changes.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.

# Session Handoff — RRC instructional video Round 7 (first-person head turn), and the demos shipped live on the RRC briefing page

## Where it started
Picked up from the Round 6 handoff. Four grill questions were answered, then he delegated the rest ("I give you the liberty to do this in the best way possible").
